````markdown
## Requirements and actual implementation inspected

Frozen SC-031 expected effect: “Ingen negativ APERAK enbart för dessa extrauppgifter; uppgifterna påverkar inte affärsdata.” Its prohibited effect: “Mottagningstoleransen innebär inte att den egna byggaren ska börja skicka dessa uppgifter.” P-02 requires inbound ignored business projection and retained raw payload. This candidate asserts a bounded part of the prohibited effect, without pretending to assert the inbound effects.

I independently read the full 140-line test, real `renderProdat` production policy and failure path, Z01 builder/profileRenderer, immutable PRODAT26A matrix, register policy, actual serializer/envelope adapter and tokenizer structured access. Matrix Z01 cells exclude 213/QTY31, 214/CCI Z02, 218/CCI Z05 and 224/RFF MG. Production policy direction is outbound; inbound register ignore remains distinct. Engine aggregates field issues and throws `ProdatRenderValidationError` before returning on production errors.

Both L/Z22 and LK/Z23 use real production renderer → serializer → tokenizer. Positive assertions retain message/version, own document reference, physical LIN/object agency, date, LI reference, reason, UD, optional IT and own identity. Structured NAD data retains literal `Synthetic RFF+MG User`; qualifier absence assertions therefore cannot mistake a name string for a forbidden reference. Positive IT and absent-IT contrast prevent satisfying the proof by dropping every optional party or dropping UD. Absent IT is the deliberately absent installation address branch, consistent with the real optional parent logic.

For each L/LK profile, explicit scalar and register-array annualConsumption, meterConstant and meterDigitCount are exercised. Tests require the actual production exception class plus canonical forbidden-field issue, so unrelated failures or mocked output cannot satisfy them. Caller input equality is asserted after positive rendering and each refusal. Real renderer/serializer/tokenizer are not mocked. DB `from`/`rpc` and fetch throw; no actual backend, source authority or transport is claimed. The proof's synthetic dependency selections are declared; their source strings are not treated as authorization.
````

````javascript
function deny(kind) { return (...args) => { attempts.push(kind); throw new Error('SC037_FINITE_BOUNDARY_FORBIDDEN:' + kind); }; }

for (const [kind, mod] of [['http',http],['https',https]]) for (const name of ['request','get','createServer']) mod[name] = deny(kind + '.' + name);
for (const name of ['lookup','resolve','resolve4','resolve6','reverse']) { dns[name] = deny('dns.' + name); dns.promises[name] = deny('dns.promises.' + name); }
for (const name of ['spawn','spawnSync','exec','execSync','execFile','execFileSync','fork']) childProcess[name] = deny('child_process.' + name);
````

````markdown
**An input selector locates existing evidence; it never supplies an accepted decision or legal authority.** Existing60cc/request/producer-esco0c/ce80 actors must select suitable already-authorized saved/sent originals on their disposable backend and retain original/execution custody. The prospective source should read those originals through the existing qualified paths, rather than recreate their agreement, issuer, permission, assignment, history or send.

Frozen cards: `docs/ediel/masterplan-v2/registers/acceptance_tests.json` 3208–3213 and 3256–3261. ESCO; real customer identity rather than birthdate; legal identityless Z13 with LIN+1; separate scope/role/customer and DSO agreements/beneficiary. DGI/Z13/S17 requests ongoing V, S18 bounded VH; own CONTRL and APERAK acknowledge the request. Only independently positive Z14 can establish object-related rights; no electricity supply effect. Positive request ACK is not a prerequisite imposed on lawful earlier Z14.

## Minimal producer-supplied selector packet

For each V and VH case, the owner supplies opaque locators and expected identities, not row-shaped fixture facts:

| Required owner input | What it binds; what the prospective source may read/derive |
|---|---|
| Exact execution source head/tree and disposable-backend/input-epoch binding | Owner selects the applicable coherent source/install and guards. d798 is this design's source, not automatic qualification of the owner's later carrier/backend. No backend URLs, tokens, signing keys or credentials belong in the selector contract/report. |
| `companyId`, `environment`, current scoped `actorUserId` | Fixed expected tenant/environment/principal for original reads and received-ACK processing. Existing membership/permissions/legal-role owners must actually admit that actor; an ID or `authorized: true` field cannot do so. A separately scoped adverse actor/tenant is supplied only for a specifically planned refusal control. |
| `originalMessageId`, expected original SHA256, expected mode `V` or `VH` | Pin one existing outbound PRODAT/Z13 saved/sent original. Read and compare exact original ID/company/environment/customer, immutable raw/hash and accepted-source binding; do not search “latest Z13”, retarget another successful request, mark a draft sent, or send it again. Derive expected wire reason S17/S18 from the explicit mode and compare to the own physical field223. |
| Expected `customerId`, plus existing assignment/permission binding locator or its owner-qualified selection receipt | Fixed expected customer prevents deriving both the target and oracle from an accidentally selected sibling. AssignmentId/permissionId may be read from the protected original binding and checked against the supplied locator; provider actor, DSO actor, beneficiary, versions and scope must come from their existing owners. They are distinct identities, never aliases of companyId/customerId. No duplicated raw personal identifier need be supplied when the qualified bound customer source is readable. |
| Case disposition/baseline eligibility | Owner identifies a request suitable for the intended observation. For “ACK alone grants no early rights”, read the actual baseline and establish that this particular request has no positive Z14-established rights yet. An already positively confirmed sibling or existing coexisting V must remain unchanged. Previously established rights are not removed merely to make a pending-case test. No universal ACK-before-Z14 ordering follows. |
| Reception scenario selector, if existing physical ACKs are reused | Owner supplies existing incoming ACK IDs/expected hashes and their mailbox/parse/reception locator; source reads actual immutable receipt. If the authorized later scenario will ingest a newly rendered physical reply, its ACK ID/hash/mail/parse/reception/canonical assessment are outputs of that actual intake, not selector-supplied accepted facts. The execution owner determines those inputs after source custody and reviews. |

Permission/assignment/intent/outbound-request IDs are not interchangeable: actual `prodatServicePermission.ts` 45–66 links intent operationId/customerId to the permission and stores outbound-request payload `{serviceAssignmentId, permissionId, servicePermissionCommandKey, messageCode, sourceEvidenceId}`. `metering_permissions` schema 31231–31273 distinguishes internal `id`, national `permission_id`, own LI, original Z13 link and independently received Z14/Z15 links.

## Derived observations, with no authority manufacture

- **Physical wire selectors:** tokenize the unchanged bound original. Read UNB complete sender/receiver composites, interchange reference, application reference/test environment; UNH reference/profile; BGM document reference; own first LIN position, nullable point/agency, RFF/LI and adjacent CCI/Z13+CAV/S17-or-S18. CONTRL uses own UCI interchange reference and exact original party composites (including documented first14 projection); APERAK uses own ACW document reference and own object LI with reversed legal parties. Do not replace LI with `1`, a UUID, customer ID or a fabricated installation ID. `sourceCorrelation.ts` 16–29, 98–112, 126–174 defines these scopes; null object identity with real LI is supported.
- **Customer/legal scope:** existing `ServicePermissionOriginBasis` in `permissionOrigin.ts` 9–19 separates company, assignment/scope versions, permission/state version, provider/DSO actors, legal sender/receiver, customer, mode, purpose/product/period and reviewed evidence. Read the protected binding/current applicable owner evidence and compare its original snapshot; `parsed_payload.sourcePermissionBasis` alone cannot self-authorize. The recovery-specific guard at79–84 illustrates exact binding, not a reason to create a recovery operation here. Resolve identity from qualified org_number/personal_number semantics (`customerIdentity.ts` 25–55), not identifier length, birthdate or fixture constants. Internal customer ID remains separate from physical NAD/UD identity.
- **Time:** preserve original reporting period and captured request day/network agreement bounds. `requestTiming.ts` 5–19 records immutable evidence/review/scope version and server requestDay; no fresh Date.now substitution, artificial historic permission window or request re-origination. VH original already needs its genuine bounded history terms. Original send/receipt timestamps are read from the owner-bound source; actual incoming reception clock comes from its intake. Existing policy checks remain, without a newly invented LIVE/provider-clock/blanket-wait gate.
- **Watch/transport:** read the original's recorded ACK expectations/status/deadlines and existing accepted attempt/watch identity where the current consumer exposes it. Do not invent a required watch plan, force deadlines, infer remote delivery from `status: sent` or calculate an oracle from a new helper clock. `acceptedProjection.ts` 3–16 returns exact company/environment/message/attempt/originalHash/observedAt/frozenRecipient and explicitly `authorizesProviderEntry:false`, `deliveryProven:false`; it is not permission to call a provider. Technical and business expectations remain separate. Existing plan absence/holds retain their actual qualification; G1 is the own incoming ACK witness.
- **Committed result:** actual input to `readCommittedInboundAck` is scoped actor plus the actual persisted incoming ACK (`committedInboundAck.ts` 12–23). Its output must be `exact_receipt`, own original/sourceMessageId and original bytes/hash/company/environment, actual outcome/scope/own references and unchanged replay (6–8,28–32). Legacy diagnostic, matched email, parser object, guessed canonical ID, mutable status or supplied `sourceAccepted:true` is not that receipt. Native schema correlation 97718–97738 binds ACK/source hashes, canonical assessment, actor, exact scope and outcomes; those are actual owner-written outputs, never seed inputs.

## Resume boundary and limits

Resume input is therefore: **owner-qualified source/backend epoch + scoped principal + two exact original/hash/mode/customer bindings + existing binding locators + suitable real baseline**, with optional existing ACK/reception locators for evidence reuse. LI/parties/point/profile/time/watch and downstream receipt IDs are read or derived from those bound existing sources and real owner outputs. Expected metadata is checked against authority; it does not create authority. A missing/ambiguous/stale binding stays a concrete hold, with no automatic fallback, issuer/grant insertion, permission repair or extra producer.

The G1 source should observe request ACK effects and conserve original, supply and independent assignment/permission/grant baselines. It does not need to originate a new Z13, execute an entire later Z14/history/E66 chain, require a positive ACK before Z14, claim external DSO delivery or invent a same-point supply setup. Identityless LIN+1/nullpoint is lawful and must remain representable.

The old Z13 native donors are design history only; their source creators, synthetic signed receipts and failed runs do not grant current selector authority. The actual API call order remains the separate current_delivery_review trace, not this input-design report. This design author must be treated as a source coauthor if adopted into the later source and cannot supply an independent final review of that version. Native remains NOT_RUN; no whole-card, runtime or ledger qualification is claimed.
````

````markdown
# Z13 G1 bounded source design — no authoring or execution authorization

Exact public main: `d798eb36f35cd4f437d44c8d234b4dbb81adc7a6`; tree `e3b6c11677a2b871a128504f1616def7d22dba1e`. All line references below are from this commit, read through `git show`; the shared checkout and historical remote tracking reference are not source authority.

Outcome: the existing canonical incoming-ACK APIs can qualify customer-bound identityless Z13 originals without inventing installation identity or requiring ACKs before Z14. Preparation is not execution-ready: actual selected original IDs, existing immutable origin/timing/watch/accepted-transport bindings and an authoring handoff have not been supplied to this actor. No new source defect is established by this design. A data-only descriptor cannot manufacture missing authority. Request `#7666149872726` remains pending in the parent’s latest handoff.

Custody remains unchanged: whole Z13 request `60cc652f5b97d9e8e0f741593599b0013c3ff711`, ESCO producer `0c55919a640a822d0953f68be3aec89ada3a46da`, native/workflow `ce80cdd3fb4be418ea54daebb596d798889442cd`. The proposed preparation paths are only `scripts/ediel-z13-request-ack-native-358d1615.test.ts` and `scripts/ediel-z13-request-ack-native-358d1615.config.ts`; neither is authored here. If this design is adopted, this helper is a design coauthor and cannot be counted as an independent final reviewer.

## Data-only selection and admission boundary

A prospective versioned descriptor may carry scalar locators and expected values: company/environment, execution actor, customer, assignment, permission, scope-basis/assignment version, original Z13 message, existing source intent, original payload SHA256, accepted transport attempt, captured request-timing evidence, and exact watch IDs. Select independent V and VH originals (S17/S18 respectively). These are assertions to compare against actual persisted records, not trusted JSON evidence. Reject malformed UUIDs, unsupported descriptor version, non-test environment, duplicate/conflicting source identity, mode disagreement, missing raw bytes or hash disagreement before introducing incoming messages.

For each candidate, read and verify the actual outbound PRODAT Z13 source and its company/environment/customer, immutable raw hash, real sent timestamp, private origin and permission joins. The physical original must remain identityless in the installation sense: its own LIN+1 and RFF+LI are present, object point/agency may be null, and no installation identifier may be substituted for the customer. Decode the actual wire and check its DGI application/guide, BGM Z13, S17 or S18, sender/receiver including subaddresses and qualifiers, and customer identity without adding a date of birth. Compare those values with the captured origin, permission customer and actor/DSO scope. Do not select merely by mutable `status='sent'` or descriptor-supplied customer.

Useful existing read API: `readAcceptedEdielTransportProjection({companyId,environment,actorUserId,messageId})`, `lib/ediel/transport/acceptedProjection.ts:11`. It reads `gridex_ediel_accepted_transport_projection_v1`, and returns the retained attempt, originalHash, observedAt, frozenRecipient, providerReceipt and businessExpectationPlan. Its `authorizesProviderEntry:false` and `deliveryProven:false` must remain explicit. Null is a missing-input stop. Never call the repair function at line 20 to make a selector pass.

`readServicePermissionOrigin({providerCompanyId,assignmentId,actorUserId,expectedVersion,permissionId,code:'Z13'})`, `lib/ediel/services/permissionOrigin.ts:37`, reads current authorization and validates captured timing. Its held result is a stop; it does not by itself prove the immutable source-to-origin binding of a previously sent original. `loadServicePermissionMessageOrigin` at line 90 is a recovery loader, requiring `source_operation_id`; it can legitimately return undefined for an ordinary original. It is not the missing general read API for a normal sent Z13. Do not call `reserveServicePermissionOrigin` at line 44 or produce an `authorized` basis in test code.

The ordinary original-to-origin/timing/watch join is backed by schema tables `gridex_service_permission.origins` (schema line 103777), `request_timing_receipts` (103797), `gridex_service_administration.permission_request_owners` (103747), immutable `gridex_business_expectations.bindings` (98676), and public expectation rows (112510). There is no inspected single exported TypeScript reader that returns all these ordinary-source bindings. The existing qualified owner must supply the actual prepared selectors and authorize an existing read-only native query/reader for those bindings; this actor must not silently invent such a reader or seed private rows. Existing transport predicates `accepted_source_basis_v1` (17521) and `sent_source_is_current_v1` (28904) explain why a mutable sent projection alone is insufficient.

Captured request timing must already exist and belong to the selected company/assignment/permission/scope version. Use its retained request day, network period, reviewed evidence identity/hash/version and recorded proof. A new wall-clock date or fabricated watched receipt is not a replacement. Check the stored business expectation’s source/hash/transport attempt and plan; request ACKs must not fulfill a Z14/Z14N business-response watch.

For a first-pair positive witness, require a genuinely pending permission/source with no earlier complete pair, no Z14/Z15 activation, permission version zero and no granted object rights, approved market period or supply effect. Existing completed sources are replay evidence only. Keep fresh refusal cohorts separate from a positive source that has legitimately advanced to waiting.

## Existing canonical incoming path and exact calls

The existing ACK-native example is `scripts/ediel-at-z09e-received-ack-native.test.ts:57–98`; it supplies a usable ACK-layer call order, not a Z13 proof. Existing H public birth is `scripts/helpers/nationalRescissionNative.ts:136–152`. The ESCO helper `ownAcks` in `scripts/ediel-at-z14vh-z15v-esco-native.test.ts:72–99` reads outbound acknowledgements for received Z14/Z15. That is the opposite direction and cannot witness a received pair for a sent Z13. `readOutboundAckOriginals` likewise requires an inbound source and reads its outgoing ACKs.

1. Obtain owner-declared counterparty fixture bytes bound to the selected original. If synthetic response bytes are explicitly authorized, the existing renderers plus `EdifactEnvelopeCodec` can derive them from the actual immutable wire: mirror UNB parties, qualifiers and subaddresses, preserve application/environment, use fresh incoming interchange/message IDs, and correlate CONTRL UCI and APERAK ACW with the original’s actual references. APERAK must retain the original’s own LI. A descriptor UUID never substitutes for physical correlation.
2. `seedOriginalMailboxNative(sql,literal,{companyId,environment:'test',raw,smtpFrom,...})`, `scripts/helpers/originalMailboxNative.ts:29`, creates retained external mailbox/email input, actual parse result and raw hash. This is an eventual incoming-fixture write, not a side-effect-free selector, and is not authorized in this task. Preserve actual parser output; do not pass altered parsed authority.
3. Call the real `matchOutboundRequestForInbound({companyId,parsed,inboundEmailMessageId,parseResultId})`, `lib/inbound-mail/inboundMatcher.ts:55`. It writes match-attempt diagnostics; do not label it pure. The read-only `verifyInboundAckTransportCorrelation({companyId,environment,parsed,outboundMatch})`, `lib/inbound-mail/inboundAckTransportGuard.ts:179`, then `failClosedOutboundMatchForAck({outboundMatch,guard})` at 238 preserve transport refusal. Do not manufacture a matched result or overwrite its entity ID.
4. If tenant resolution is included, call `resolveInboundTenantFromIdentifiers(input)`, `lib/ediel/tenant/resolveInboundTenant.ts:442`, with real retained mailbox company/id and parsed UNB/subaddress/application/family/code/references, as the H path does. Require its actual resolved company to agree; do not provide synthetic resolved-tenant evidence. Preserve real metering-point matcher outcome: identityless Z13 does not justify adding a point.
5. `createInboundEdielMessage({companyId,actorUserId,environment,inboundEmailMessageId,parseResultId,parsed,outboundMatch,meteringPointMatch?,tenantResolution?})`, `lib/inbound-mail/inboundStatusUpdater.ts:313`, is the canonical message birth. It requires the real actor/parse record and communication permission. It records `recordInboundReception` itself (444–447); do not duplicate `recordOriginalMailboxNativeReception`. Require the actual returned message ID; null is a stop. Read it with `getEdielMessageById` and verify direction inbound, incoming raw/hash/mail linkage and real received time after sent time.
6. For the bounded ACK-layer scope used by the existing Z09 example, call `resolveCanonicalRuntimeDecisionWithRegistry(message)` (`lib/ediel/core/runtimeDecision.ts:806`), then `recordReceivedSourceValidation({original:message,validated:message,resolvedCompanyId,decision})`, `lib/ediel/core/receivedSourceValidationLedger.ts:23`, then `processInboundAckMessage({actorUserId,message})`, `lib/ediel/flows/inboundAckProcessing.ts:547`. Decisions and recorded receipt are actual outputs, never hardcoded accepted facts. Finally `readCommittedInboundAck({actorUserId,message})`, `lib/ediel/ack/committedInboundAck.ts:12`, must return `kind:'exact_receipt'` for the selected outbound source, company/environment and exact incoming payload hash. A legacy diagnostic or null is not a qualified receipt.

The bounded ACK-layer sequence must be labelled accordingly. If the eventual owner chooses the whole processing route instead, call `processInboundEdielMessage({actorUserId,edielMessageId})`, `lib/ediel/flows/inboundProcessing.ts:931`, once; it owns runtime validation and delegates to the ACK processor at 1256 onward. Do not preapply the narrow processor and then call the whole processor to manufacture two proofs. The whole path can generate its normal technical CONTRL for APERAK; preserve that behavior. The mailbox processor’s `applyCanonicalInboundAckStatusUpdate` call (`lib/inbound-mail/edielInboundProcessor.ts:311`) is not a substitute for protected committed-receipt proof.

## Effects, retry, refusals and exact resume condition

Snapshot actual site/object permission rights, provider/customer grants, service administration/permission effect history, supply state and Z14/Z15 business-response watches before incoming admission. Neither the pair nor its retry may activate rights or supply. The pending permission status is not expected to stay byte-identical: existing `wait_after_z13_ack_v1`, schema 32040–32142, can legitimately move a bound `z13_ready`/`z13_sent` permission to `waiting_for_customer_approval` after the complete positive own-source/own-LI pair. The guard requires actual captured origin, unchanged raw hash, current permission/customer/DSO bindings, no earlier complete aggregate, no activation and version zero. Source ACK projections/outbound-request acknowledgment can also legitimately change. Assert no new grant/activation/supply, while allowing those specified protocol changes.

Replay the same stored incoming message IDs through the selected processor. Check identical protected correlation/receipt count and aggregate, immutable original bytes, stable rights/supply and no additional permission effect. Do not create fresh emails and call them replay. Refusals must use distinct physical wrong UCI/ACW, own LI, mirrored parties or real company/environment/actor mismatches, with the actual matcher/runtime/SQL guard deciding. Mail, parse, diagnostics, tasks and normal technical ACK staging may be created on refusal; do not impose zero writes across all tables or overwrite a manual-review decision. No receipt/acceptance for the wrong source and no rights/supply change are the meaningful limits.

No mandatory ACK-before-Z14 gate is introduced. Request ACK acceptance is separate from later positive Z14 business authorization, and this bounded G1 design cannot establish Z14/Z14N, full SC/Z13 or current native/GEN/CI proof.

Minimal prospective config: import the existing ESCO native config unchanged, spread its config/test and replace only `include` with the new single test path. That inherits `ediel-sc014-technical-intake-native.config.ts:2,7–14` and `ediel-source-owner-native.config.ts` local CLI-status/local URL guard, aliases, permission-catalog setup and serial runtime. Do not change those guards, setup, workflow, global include or reservation ownership. Do not import/execute ESCO `seedNativeEscoFixture`, `qualifyNativeEscoFixture`, `approveAndRequest` or active-V qualifier to fill missing selected originals: those perform source/admission/grant setup outside data-only G1 scope.

Resume only after a concrete owner handoff identifies already-qualified pending V and VH originals, their immutable timing/watch/transport/origin read access and actual execution actor/cohorts, plus explicit scope for the two preparation files. Missing/held ordinary-source bindings remain an external-input/API boundary. Neither this report nor its locators authorize native execution or source writing.

Evidence limits: public source and the supplied public support/scout reports only; no private input/original/log/archive acquisition, source/config/ref/shared-document mutation, test/backend/native/GEN/CI execution, claim, foreign-thread contact or publication. No old donor originals were reread. This report is design support, not a passing result.
````

````markdown
## Ordinary target B and retained H sibling A

`scripts/ediel-at-z05-supplier-native.test.ts:63–68` adds an explicit `independent=false` selector. Only the positive unlinked L case at 335–337 passes `!linked`; linked L, LK and every other unchanged caller keep the original factory path. `scripts/helpers/ediel-national-end-native-fixture.ts:32–41` still builds/sends the actual national H original first. Its optional preparation callback is absent by default; the merge of a prepared target changes the incoming end’s customer/point/period/start source, while the H original/mandate/history remain present in the result. It does not globally relabel H as ordinary L or change the native SQL end guard.

The new helper’s `prepareIndependentNationalEndTarget(base,provider)`, line 193, retains A before preparing B. `retainNationalSibling`, lines 45–126, fixes A’s pre-B message-ID set and snapshots complete selected graph, role/membership/permission, tenant/legal/profile, source/mail, mandate/capability, transport/outbox and watch rows. A must be a real sent H original with its actual mandate and pending Z05 watch. A’s snapshot is compared after multiple B setup steps and before/after processing and replay (test 338–346, 351, 417). New B messages cannot enter the closed A set to hide a prebaseline mutation.

B gets a separate scoped operator, not added grants on A. Lines 199–211 invoke the existing `createBilateralSourceOperator`, check the new actor differs, assert it is not in `admin_users`, and verify actual company/contract permissions. The existing operator helper (24–38) creates a disposable authenticated company member and role, then checks its real tenant context. `onboardCustomerGraph` at 229–238 creates separate customer/site/point/contract IDs with create-only semantics. Lines 239–250 sign/archive the actual B contract and reread the existing network artifact. Shared network/legal actor state is read and retained; it is not replaced with a caller-authored authorized JSON result.

The helper queues and sends B’s actual Z03 through `prepareAndQueueEdielZ03` and `sendEdielMessageViaSmtp` (315–322), checks retained original/current accepted transport, then constructs B’s physical Z04 input before immutable birth. `acceptOrdinaryStart`, 128–189, checks the own point, LI, UD identity and declared RKv1.7 register chain (101, counter constant1/digits6) in the actual prospective raw. The shared synthetic111 default is not modified. Mail/source birth is followed by actual runtime resolution, validation receipt, fresh rule evidence and received-source owner session; `applyInboundBusinessStateMachine` and `session.finish()` must produce the accepted ordinary supply period. There is no directly seeded accepted validation, protected H capability, effect receipt or admitted supply period in this new helper. Lines 325–334 assert B differs from A and has zero H operations/mandates for B’s point/period.

**Fixture-authority limit:** a blanket statement that this helper seeds no authority inputs would be false. It explicitly seeds synthetic issuer registry/representation rows (261–262), contract-bound signed masterdata declarations (277–282), requested-method declarations (287–292), and a public ready switch request with declared caller invoicee selection (313). These are the same categories used by the retained normal-switch fixture (253/268/278). The BRP archive/review is performed through actual authenticated APIs, and the source/period admission is performed by canonical owners. This supports mechanism testing with declared disposable legal/issuer/masterdata boundaries; it establishes neither genuine customer authentication nor authentic external legal approval. No protected final owner result is manually stamped to bypass the consumer, and this review does not broaden that limited proof.

## Assertions, ACKs, guard and replay preservation

Static reconstruction reversed only the three added imports, explicit independent-selector parameter/callback, C1 supplement, changed positive unlinked fixture argument and three sibling checks. The remaining test bytes equal prior894 exactly. Both original and reconstructed SHA256 are `97260d1446dd33d1a37d24540dd3bd6748ef825fc61a68e7133fc7a1f89ff3ec`; all **110 original `expect(` calls** remain byte-identical. This is a source integrity check, not test execution.

The unchanged config `scripts/ediel-z05-supplier-native.config.ts` still includes the retained20 H/bilateral originals,4 LK controls and this file’s15 existing registrations: **39 retained prospective cases plus1 new C1 case =40**, not40PASS. Both included companion test files and `nationalRescissionNative.ts`, normal-switch fixture and source-owner config are byte-identical to prior894. The schema/migrations are also unchanged between894 ande49; base-main composition is a separate previously reviewed whole-source concern.

The positive body still asserts actual period/source/version/end/history, scoped ordinary protected effect receipt, unchanged H watch, only the relevant final-metering task, zero additional outbound PRODAT, unchanged unrelated graph and exact original/start/end bytes. Physical outbound CONTRL UCI mirror and APERAK100/Z07/LI/ACW checks remain at 372–394. Replay at411–416 preserves actual receipt/task/ACK and graph snapshots. Defaults still require the H-own LI; the corrected ordinary cohort uses an independent own notice LI on distinct B. Existing negative consumer/SQL checks at469–483 and the unchanged native `has_end_selector_v1`/`matched_end_v1`/exact-sent-original guard (schema32393/32444/32650,44330) are not weakened. This review does not claim a newly executed H-wrong-LI case or add one.

## New C1 observer: real calls and truthful bounded output

The supplement at200–333 reads catalog/function definition/prosrc hash, runtime string setting/encoding/collation and indexes. It retains the old two-backslash SQL function body as a hash-checked scalar SELECT expression, never replacing the installed function. The declared VALUES-only aliases show old and current normalized outputs/candidate sets diverge, and assert the equivalence oracle rejects that divergence (267–273). No accepted owner rows are inserted by this counterexample.

`readDomain`, 223–241, scans the complete current `platform_grid_owners` universe and emits universe count/hash plus all old/current/lowercase match candidates. `sameRelation` at254–258 compares complete unrestricted helper sets, complete active/Ediel/OPS importer canonical sets, and the helper holds/importer count/min-ID/lowercase-fallback choice. `choice` at242–253 deliberately keeps JS trim for helpers separate from SQL btrim for importer eligibility. The underlying helper queries at `ediel-z02-supplier-native-fixture.ts:342–346,423–426` explicitly aggregate `ORDER BY id`; array order is therefore defined. The importer at schema73325–73348 reuses only a unique canonical match, otherwise falls back to lower(name) upsert. Candidate counts and fallback choices are covered, rather than an empty-domain or scalar-key equality claim.

The SQL observer captures the original `nativeSql` function before installing a spy, delegates each original query unchanged and returns its exact result. Additional before/after SELECTs call the captured function directly, so they do not recursively alter the observed query. It records operand provenance from actual source-grid snapshots and checks the returned actual ordered IDs (273–292). It adds observational assertions; it is not a universally transparent pass for arbitrary mismatched state.

The RPC observer captures the bound original function once (203), delegates unrelated RPCs directly and passes the selected importer’s original argument references unchanged (296–301). `Promise.resolve(actualRpc(...args))` consumes the selected original PostgREST thenable once; the continuation returns the same response object, with actual error intact (301–307). The real caller `lib/energy/resolver.ts:1377–1385` awaits that RPC directly, so it relies on neither fluent chaining nor original builder identity. Importer-entry/exit assertions inspect actual results and preserve errors; no mock success replaces the output. `finally` restores both spies (332).

Each actual L/LK setup is required to reach helper343 once. The actual known-area path either reuses a valid reference with no query/import calls, or reaches the importer; at least one genuine importer invocation is required across the supplement (311–329). This ensures a prospective run can refuse missing reachability. It cannot be credited before actual terminal output.

The emitted boundary at330–331 is accurate: `THIS_L_LK_SETUP_AND_KNOWN_AREA_SUPPLEMENT_ONLY`, historical113 domain not retained/reconstructed, current113 not run, no universal alias equivalence. Matching current helper relation despite different scalar keys does not prove unchanged historical113 results or all omitted phases. Public6150340040 correctly withdraws unconditional old113 applicability and retains genuine old113 only at its original source. Existing owner must separately qualify actual current40 and omitted-phase name-state/source applicability, or obtain affected113 under its existing custody; this support report creates no new review gate and infers no PASS.

## Findings and completion boundary

Confirmed introduced defects: none in this bounded inspection. Refuted concerns: lost assertions, broad default-H replacement, manually inserted protected accepted source/period/H results, unordered helper arrays, duplicate selected RPC execution, and C1 universal/historical113 equivalence claims. # AT-Z03C: actual queued-send calendar boundary
````

````markdown
READ_ONLY source support, 2026-10-13. Actual main `d798eb36f35cd4f437d44c8d234b4dbb81adc7a6` / tree `e3b6c11677a2b871a128504f1616def7d22dba1e`; public #795 `e49a23d4897f1cb4063a57bf3b1c810321723c3a` / tree `88c04102169c9bd803e2bf4dfb7363ebbc562097`. No test/native/backend/GEN/CI/log/original/private-proposal acquisition or source/ref/claim/writer action. Only this temporary report. Postgres locking/security guidance applied; no schema optimization or gate relaxation proposed.

**Result: no newly confirmed C queued-send productbug.** The “preparation-only cutoff” hypothesis is refuted by actual prepare/enter source checks. The simple “last context keeps a pre-contract-lock day” hypothesis is refuted by earlier customer-source contexts acquiring those same locks and the later C context refreshing its day. A narrower UTC+1-versus-Stockholm calendar-domain question is concrete source evidence, but its normative timezone boundary is unresolved by the frozen card/timer text; it must not be promoted to a demonstrated late-send defect.

## Literal authority and clocks

- Frozen `acceptance_tests.json:2904–2909`: own original/case/object/customer; L no later than four days before, LK no later than moving day; own DDQ/Z03/Z24 cancellation, ACKs and applicable Z04C; SMTP250 alone does not complete cancellation.
- Frozen `timers.json:80–99`, TM-Z03C-L/LK: anchors supply_start/move_in_day, **calendar_days**, -4 days / latest moving day, HB26A197–199. These rows do not specify the day-boundary timezone.
- Frozen ENV-08 (`rules.json:518–520`) governs **payload** fixed standard time; `prodat_fields.json:350/357` governs field206 fixed UTC+1. This is evidence for wire conversion, not an independently explicit definition of cancellation send-calendar timezone.
- Actual guide-derived `deadlinePolicy.ts:88–91` maps the same HB197 C constraints; its pure C projection at307–319 declares calendar `Europe/Stockholm`. This is an existing implementation declaration, not a new frozen normative clause or automatic proof that every transport owner consumes it.
- Actual C SQL context uses timezoneUTC and initializes `today := (clock_timestamp()+interval '1 hour')::date` at schema32818/32822 (original migration20260930214933:23/26). It is wallclock sampled once per function invocation, **not transaction now()**. Immutable original start conversion remains separate from that execution-day sample.

## Reached ordinary source/provider path

1. Genuine C owner derives cancellation from sealed accepted own normal Z03. Effective context schema32837–32866 checks native original/hash, own Z22-or-Z23/LI/customer/point/grid/current supplier and unchanged case/agreement/site. It derives `start_day` from the immutable own source; deadline is **Z22=start_day-4, Z23=start_day**. Same original start must equal the persisted request date. Source window is enforced at32866; the returned basis includes originalSubtype/deadline at32876. Caller C/Z24 metadata cannot choose a different source subtype/window.
2. `sendEdielMessageViaSmtp`, `transport/index.part-2.ts:412–427`, executes actual registry send admission (`sendGuards.ts:27–38`, mode='send', persisted messageRow). There is no `admitEdielOutgoingWire` symbol in either inspected public tree; the actual path is named above. `validator.ts:162–176/586–606/686–706` captures fresh admission and resolves the actual registry rule pack. `messagePolicy.ts:62–89` separates fresh outbound admission from physical document/period clocks, using Stockholm date for the guide. Source-wide callers show no `canonicalSupplierSwitchSendPolicy` C evaluation in this transport/registry path; the pure helper is reached by its facade/projections, not used here to compare original C deadline. `dateEventContext.ts:21–23` excludes Z03, so it cannot silently add that missing C original-subtype check.
3. `outboundAttempt.ts:57–88` runs date-current guard before prepare, after prepare and **after enter**. The final check compares the recorded admission.referenceDate with current Stockholm guide day; it does not compare C originalSubtype/start/deadline. `sendEdielEmail.ts:55–56/88–91` awaits that callback before the actual provider invocation. A midnight crossing in the guide clock therefore refuses provider entry and requires full admission again.
4. Each RPC reaches `public.gridex_ediel_transport_attempt_v1` schema66154 onward and actual `gridex_ediel_transport.mutate_v1`18502–18505. Graph/source locks and the existing sealed message/current actor/attempt reservation/worker fence remain. The lower core writes entered state18352–18354, but later wrappers execute within the same transaction and can still refuse/roll back that result before the RPC returns.
5. Effective `mutate_before_production_contract_source_v1:18106–18114` calls `ediel_require_switch_cancellation_source_current_v1` on **both prepare and enter when proceed=true**. Guard49083–49112 rechecks exact sealed C private origin, current native own normal-source context, intent/request and original method; context is invoked at49105. This is actual current-send source qualification, not solely preparer validation. Ordinary queued C that starts after the existing SQL calendar deadline is held.

## Repeated contexts and lock-order refutation

Do not infer a bug from context's entry-time `today` alone. Actual outer `mutate_before_requested_method_entry_v1:18221–18234` prelocks customer message sources and calls `gridex_customer_masterdata.require_current_v1(...,'send')` **before** delegating to lower transport mutations. For genuine cancellation preparation:

- require_current schema9046–9048 calls `require_cancellation_preparation_v1`9001–9023.
- That invokes `customer_draft_v1`9013 → `customer_source_v1`32893 → context32957; customer_draft then invokes context again32894 after customer_source has finished.
- Each context obtains own original/switch/contract/point/site locks (32826–32836/32850–32852). The subsequent C transport guard49105 invokes context yet again.

Consequently, a proposed contract/point wait inside the **first** context is followed by a new context after those locks are held. A wait released after the fixed+1 rollover makes the subsequent context see the new day and reject an expired deadline. The final-context contract/point-lock example cannot simply assume those locks were not already obtained by the actual customer-source entry guard. This refutes that specific cached-day example; it does not establish a blanket absence of every possible late outer lock.

Complete effective wrapper-call order was inspected through the current mutate chain, including pre-source/customer entry, actual C guard, late consumed-record/bilateral/registry/reference/duplicate guards. Registry transport check occurs later (`mutate_before_prodat_unused_document_v1:18097–18098` → `gridex_registry_import.require_message_market_v1:30302–30320`, with SHARE table locks at30303). No later C context invocation was found after those outer guards. However a bare late-lock statement is not an independent reached failure: some locks may already be held, graph serialization may order a legitimate competing owner before the C sample, and final Stockholm guide-date checking can reject a cross-day result. No newly confirmed outer-lock C race is claimed here.

## Narrower clock-domain hypothesis, not normative productbug proof

During DST the fixedUTC+1 cancellation-day rollover and Europe/Stockholm guide-day rollover differ. For deadline D=2026-10-13:

- At22:30UTC, the SQL current day is D, while Stockholm/guide day is D+1. All freshly repeated C contexts can still accept `deadline=D`; a final guide check against a freshly admitted D+1 guide is unchanged. Pure C policy's declared Stockholm calendar would call that day expired, but that pure projection is not the reached transport cutoff owner.
- In the proposed22:59:59→23:00:01UTC example, SQL day changes D→D+1 while Stockholm guide stays D+1. Thus the **guide-date comparison alone** cannot prove freshness of the fixed+1 C day. Yet the repeated actual C context after a contract wait can reject it, as above. A different genuinely last post-context wait still needs an independently demonstrated admissible call/lock ordering before being called a productbug.
- In winter, when both clocks roll at23UTC, the final guide-date guard rejects a crossing even if a prior SQL sample were stale. A generic “cached clock means late provider send” extension is false.

This report establishes an actual calendar-domain implementation difference and missing standalone transport consumption of the pure C policy; it does **not** establish from ENV-08 or an implementation-only timezone label which day boundary HB197 normatively mandates for C send. No time-of-day cutoff borrowed from BRP, no four-day real-time wait, production clock override, new LIVE condition or all-negative native gate follows.

## Timely sent ACK and accepted replay

The fresh-send C guard is applied only to prepare/enter with proceed=true (`18112–18113`). Source-qualified positive/negative TGT branches in49085–49098 explicitly authorize no production cancellation/business effect and are not a fallback for an expired genuine C request. Existing accepted/replayed attempt returns proceed=false rather than authorizing a new provider call; SQL guard is then skipped and TS accepted-projection path (`outboundAttempt.ts:75–80`) returns the recorded provider observation. The actual customer entry wrapper also delegates established attempt states18228–18231 before fresh source checks. This must remain historical/no-resend behavior.

Repository-wide callsite inspection found no incoming-ACK call to `ediel_require_switch_cancellation_source_current_v1`; other context calls are C source/reservation/bind/customer prepare/send functions. Do not introduce this outgoing deadline as a new rejection condition for a timely sent original's later physically correlated ACK or stored replay. ACK receipt/original/hash/current actor/source authority remain with their existing owners; this narrow audit neither requalifies native31 nor approves whole C03.

## Existing assertions and minimal retained-owner follow-up

Read, not rerun: `scripts/ediel-switch-cancellation-sql-regression.mjs:59–61` distinguishes L+3 expired and LK-1 expired; exact-source/refusal/replay assertions preserve own origin. `__tests__/ediel-switch-cancellation-source.test.ts:44` preserves fixedUTC+1 **payload start** in summer, not a send-calendar deadline oracle. These do not prove a DST-boundary/post-last-lock provider test, but no new native execution is mandated merely because it is absent.

Next useful owner action: adjudicate the exact **send-calendar** timezone from its already qualified HB197/TM authority, keeping fixedUTC+1 payload conversion unchanged, then bind an explicit original-subtype/deadline/day-domain oracle to the existing reached guard. Two fresh independent source refutations should first challenge (a) the repeated-context/lock-order analysis and (b) whether an actual earlier transport admission owner already supplies a normative C cutoff. If timezone/currentness correction is actually justified after that, route only a bounded source/forward/component through retained C cancellation/serial/GEN custody; refresh the chosen current day after relevant waits and preserve accepted replay/later-ACK separation. No claim, correction implementation, new producer or whole-chain gate has been made by this support.

## Byte pins and limits

All listed file bytes are equal d798↔e49; entire schemas are not asserted equal:

- C origination migration20260930214933 SHA256 `633c87d3109963fd1535b7b1e371303e5d65f764c2ad59ed81febc461ab91163`.
- Original-method forward20261007132500 `9f64ac10d037d5084520d22706fa3d351621d091f5ece54ad8896105223e4086`.
- Customer preparation forward20261009082553 `d1d5ed8a3731f281826de39e4ebd4868a3090016dfef4544d6964352242cf351`.
- outboundAttempt `9736a44959de820a5a923cd89c29b405d182d4256665c0fb2d65e2a824bc217b`; sendEdielEmail `8a2850527daa3f8f95671860150d47b1268467911fe7791fc250c812aed3011b`.
- deadlinePolicy `12ec133d44866aaf4cffae628ee74def5d811a5a07ac12e8cf49e40ac1c24dbf`; messagePolicy `6539b87ff3cfdabfc85ed2378026704f8018bde9063dacfe6a2869b95d1d1aa1`.
- Registry sendGuards `f70b22c76a3d25099422fb839b05ea21cc41b9cec81be45540539e2cd8157a0f`; validator `be78da306d7efa75208a1c29c76e797928f311e1c1849078c2df37fe58a44018`.
- Frozen timers `f00f75cf30363b127c9e0dbfeb21106973351bd0891404789b2afb96f0b48c38`.

Effective schema blocks (CREATE FUNCTION through next CREATE, including intervening dump comments) also match: context `96f0855f1dd54977c4124330e7ade57bf4ca931ac2b5d1580d622c552fd80846`; current C guard `6959b6519d190922403bcc0c975b5d298e30c00e6eb78a87800957ee68ad8949`; actual C transport wrapper `2511ab422894883ee84031bae8f1cc239878528911b9830cfa873c99c2c616f4`; outer mutate `e8f2acc6c77e58127e58f9a7e6f25ae94fc80cd8c6f2ac5097c035f433a9dedf`.

No measured late send/concurrency failure, no original outcome qualification and no approval count. Existing C03 whole adjudication/cancellation31 producer/collector/source owners are retained. Original ediel-helper-20261013-z03c-queued-window-refutation-a.md, 14844 bytes, SHA256 `775a393dfbc06882cd9395228c31a4b8a87a4e9aa115191a739336d097cfa676`; preserved verbatim:
````

````markdown
# Independent Z03C queued-window refutation A

**The specified own-switch/contract/point/site/intent lock-wait escape is REFUTED by the actual entry call graph.** A later cancellation-source context is recomputed within the same RPC transaction after the earlier checks have obtained those locks. The separate summer fixed-UTC+1 versus Stockholm date-domain difference is visible in source, but this review does not establish which calendar zone the frozen C contract prescribes and does not confirm a product defect. Fresh canonical guide admission is not independently shown to enforce the C deadline policy.

Exact public source: `e49a23d4897f1cb4063a57bf3b1c810321723c3a`, tree `88c04102169c9bd803e2bf4dfb7363ebbc562097`; actual-main comparison `d798eb36f35cd4f437d44c8d234b4dbb81adc7a6`, tree `e3b6c11677a2b871a128504f1616def7d22dba1e`. All inspected transport/validator/source-policy files and listed cancellation SQL function fragments are byte-identical between these versions; whole schema line numbers differ where unrelated prior candidate migrations change layout. This is a new narrow source-only audit, not a repetition of the old C03 whole31/basic-deadline review.

Independent reviewer A consulted no source-agent report or peer B. Scope is ordinary genuine source-bound C cancellation, not source-qualified certification fixtures, private DB05 state, arbitrary generic wall-clock expiry, BRP time-of-day, or an executed concurrency witness. No test, backend, SQL, native, GEN, CI, original/log/archive/private-input acquisition, reservation/ref/code/shared-document change or publication occurred. Sole output is this report. If a later correction adopts this diagnosis, conservatively exclude this diagnosis author from its independent final reviewers.

## Actual clocks and frozen requirement

`gridex_switch_cancellations.context_v1`, schema32815–32878, sets session `TimeZone='UTC'` and initializes `today := (clock_timestamp()+interval '1 hour')::date` at function entry (32822). Deadline is actual original start day minus4 for Z22/L or that day for Z23/LK; the held check is at32864–32866. This really is a cached fixed UTC+1 calendar date in each invocation; inspecting that declaration alone would support the proposed suspicion.

`stockholmBusinessDate`, `lib/ediel/core/executionContext.ts:197–205`, uses `Europe/Stockholm`. `assertEdielAdmissionDateCurrent`, `lib/ediel/transport/outboundAttempt.ts:37–40`, compares the retained canonical guide/admission `referenceDate` with that current Stockholm date. The gate calls it before prepare, after prepare and after awaiting enter (60/83/88). It checks admission-date continuity, not the original C deadline itself.

The frozen `AT-Z03C-SUPPLIER` row states own original/point/customer, L no later than4days before, LK no later than move-in day, cancellation requested rather than complete from SMTP250, and no wrong-role/direction/correlation/mutation execution. It names no explicit timezone. The canonical deadline catalogue `deadlinePolicy.ts:88–91` models those offsets, while `canonicalSupplierSwitchSendPolicy` chooses `calendar:'Europe/Stockholm'` at307–319. Payload field206/ENV-08 declares year-round UTC+1; that does not automatically specify every business-process calendar. The frozen masterplan expressly requires process-specific date boundaries and rejects blanket global Stockholm conversion of all payload values (MASTERMASTERPLAN_v2.md:415). Consequently this review does not resolve the C calendar’s normative zone from a payload offset or merely from implementation metadata.

## Ordinary C provider path and decisive repeated context

A well-formed Z03C reaches `sendEdielMessageViaSmtp` in `transport/index.part-2.ts`. Existing accepted-journal replay returns at352–359 before new send admission. Fresh send loads actual immutable customer/source context at383, validates the canonical registry at414 and captures its guide referenceDate at425. `sendCorrectionFencedEmail` chooses ordinary generic transport for a physical Z03 rather than the Z08 lane (`sources/correctionOutboundDispatch.ts:37–44`). The generic callback requires actual prepare/enter RPC success before the provider is called; an RPC error propagates and cannot authorize I/O.

`public.gridex_ediel_transport_attempt_v1`, schema66252–66259, requires service role and calls `gridex_ediel_transport.mutate_v1`. The latter acquires the current graph/source-receipt locks and traverses the real nested mutator chain (18502–18505). The key ordering is:

1. Outer `mutate_before_requested_method_entry_v1` (18209–18234) obtains customer-source/message/reservation locks and calls `gridex_customer_masterdata.require_current_v1(...,'send')` **before** delegating inward (18233 then18234). A normal prepared C cannot skip this via the already-entered/observed branch; that branch is no fresh provider admission.
2. `require_current_v1` (9031–9051) calls actual cancellation prelock and locks the message (9035–9036), reads the existing protected preparation, then invokes `require_cancellation_preparation_v1` (9048).
3. `require_cancellation_preparation_v1` calls `customer_draft_v1` (9013). That calls `customer_source_v1` (32893), which calls context at32957; then `customer_draft_v1` calls context **again** at32894. Context locks the own original (32827), own switch (32836), signed contract, metering point and site (32850–32852). After the second context, draft additionally locks the actual origin, intent and outbound request (32895–32897). These transaction locks persist when the outer mutator continues. `customer_source_v1` also invokes current masterdata/source checks; its recursion is to the ordinary original’s preparation, not another unchecked cancellation authority.
4. The inward shared transport core obtains actual message/reservation/attempt/worker locks, validates authority and sets entered state (18260–18354). Additional mandatory-intent validation acquires intent/profile/route/point locks (18793–18834). There is still no SMTP call inside SQL.
5. While unwinding, `mutate_before_production_contract_source_v1` calls `ediel_require_switch_cancellation_source_current_v1` after its inner delegate returns proceed=true (18111–18113). This is a **new** check, not reuse of an earlier JSON context. At49195 it invokes `context_v1(...,false)` again; at49196–49197 it rereads/locks intent and outbound request and at49201 rejects a held/changed source. `false` separates current send from prepare execution; it does not disable the calendar check. The own switch/contract/point/site/intent/original locks named in the hypothesis are already retained from steps1–3.

The prelock functions at33036/33050 obtain graph and ordered original/customer-source message locks and check the source epoch. They do not themselves lock every own switch/contract/site/intent row. The refutation therefore relies on the demonstrated earlier canonical preparation/source calls and later fresh context, not an inaccurate assertion that the first prelock acquired everything.

Suppose deadline day is D and the first context starts in summer at22:59:59UTC. Its fixed+1 date is D. If its own switch/contract/point/site/intent lock wait returns at23:00:01UTC, that first cached check can still pass. But the later context at49195 initializes a new fixed+1 date D+1 while those rows are already locked, returns held at32866, and causes `switch_cancellation_current_source_held` at49201. Any lower entered-state writes in this same RPC transaction roll back. The RPC cannot return proceed=true; the TS callback cannot enter SMTP. Stockholm’s unchanged D+1 guide date over those two seconds is therefore irrelevant to this specific escape. This refutes the proposed named-lock schedule without asserting that every conceivable delay after the final check is harmless.

## Separate summer date-domain hypothesis

At22:30UTC in summer on day D, fixed UTC+1 date is D while Stockholm date is D+1. A fresh SQL context can regard a deadlineD C as still in its window. The guide-date guard can also remain stable atD+1 before/after enter. Recomputing SQL context does not remove this distinct calendar-domain disagreement.

I traced the actual transport validator to avoid treating the pure deadline projection as a live blocker: `index.part-2.ts:414` -> `assertRegistryRulebookAllowsSend` (`sendGuards.ts:27–39`) -> `validateRulebookMessageWithRegistry`/`policyForValidation` (`validator.ts:346–388,444–478,687–698`). The resulting policy selects current guide/subtype/field/registered source requirements. The PRODAT policy construction (`canonicalEdielPolicy.ts:286–310`) contains no supplier cancellation deadline evaluation. No call from this traced validator/source-gate path to `canonicalSupplierSwitchSendPolicy`, its C `cancellationOfSubtype`, or the operations cancellation scheduler was found. The actual C deadline-policy callers are the canonical facade projection and `operations/supplierSwitchScheduler.ts` (44,189–192,237–245), not a demonstrated fresh generic-send gate.

The genuine original subtype/start are available to the SQL owner in original wire/context (32842–32866) and to `readSwitchCancellationSource` for preparing C. Fresh customer masterdata send context (`sendValidationSources.ts:19–20`; `customerMasterdataSource.ts:39–47`) obtains actual canonical source approval through `ediel_customer_masterdata_message_basis_v1` (45930–45938), which invokes current C context checks. Its returned typed projection contains customer/address/source metadata; it is not a call to the pure C deadline policy carrying `cancellationOfSubtype` and deadline. A detached row/intent C/Z24 marker is likewise not proof that this immutable-original-dependent deadline evaluation occurs inside the outgoing field validator.

Thus the simpler hypothesis is **not refuted by a proven Stockholm C-deadline gate**. It remains an implementation date-domain inconsistency to adjudicate against the process-specific normative calendar, not a confirmed late-send product bug or current native failure. The frozen source inspected here does not establish the missing normative zone, and no genuine controlled-clock/native/locking proof was executed. Do not label it P2, alter a calendar, run a new producer or add a merge gate solely from this preliminary support report.

## Historical behavior and next boundary

Retain the historical accepted-transport replay at transport352–359 and ordinary no-resend behavior (`outboundAttempt.ts:75–79`); a later deadline cannot relabel an accepted original or authorize another provider entry. Timely sent C may receive a later ACK without a new send-window admission: this audit targets fresh provider entry only, not receipt/replay/business-response retroactive refusal. This report supplies no reason to change those semantics.

Conclusion: **REFUTE** the proposed cached-date escape through the named own-row waits. **UNRESOLVED SOURCE CALENDAR INCONSISTENCY**, with no confirmed product finding, for the independent22:30summer zone hypothesis. A future finding needs process-specific calendar authority and a complete actual applicable path/controlled witness under the existing qualified owner. Current tests/native/GEN/CI and whole C03 closure remain outside this review.

## Exact source/function byte pins

All listed files/functions are byte-identical main d798 -> publice49. Function hashes below cover the exact CREATE FUNCTION fragment through the following schema separator, including its retained newline; they do not assert that the entire schemas are identical.
````

````markdown
# Independent narrow Z03C queued-cutoff refutation B

**Outcome: REFUTE the stated own-lock/cache midnight-crossing finding. Separately, a reachable calendar-domain inconsistency is present in source but its frozen-contract impact is NOT_ESTABLISHED; no confirmed product bug or source correction is authorized by this preliminary review.**

Reviewer `/root/sc031_outbound_peer_a` performed fresh read-only analysis without consulting the source agent report or peer A. This is preliminary refutation/qualification, not code or wholeC03 approval. Exact actual main `d798eb36f35cd4f437d44c8d234b4dbb81adc7a6` / tree `e3b6c11677a2b871a128504f1616def7d22dba1e`; public source `e49a23d4897f1cb4063a57bf3b1c810321723c3a` / tree `88c04102169c9bd803e2bf4dfb7363ebbc562097`. All relevant SQL blocks and TS file blobs below independently equal across these sources.

## 1. Actual fresh transport graph refutes early-cache-as-final-authority

C context really initializes `today := (clock_timestamp()+interval '1 hour')::date` at function entry, before its own row locks (schema32822). It checks L original reasonZ22 deadline=startDay-4 or LKZ23 deadline=startDay at32864–32866. That local observation alone is not the complete reachable transport graph.

Fresh prepared/enter path `gridex_ediel_transport.mutate_before_requested_method_entry_v1` prelocks customer source/message and reservation at18223–18230. For a genuinely fresh attempt (prepared reservation, not alreadyentered/observed), it runs `gridex_customer_masterdata.require_current_v1(...,'send')` at18233 **before delegating** to lower transport wrappers. Existing entered/observed replay branch delegates without new authority; lower attempt state prevents a new proceed/provider call.

`require_current_v1` at9036 prelocks cancellation lineage/message and reads the real sealed customer preparation; at9052 it invokes `require_cancellation_preparation_v1`. A lawful actual cancellation private origin requires its matching cancellation preparation; a missing preparation does not bypass this guard. That calls `customer_draft_v1` at9013.

The actual refresh sequence is:

1. `customer_draft_v1` prelocks own original/message/source lineage at32892.
2. It calls `customer_source_v1` at32893. That calls `context_v1` at32957; then locks/qualifies original preparation and runs actual original customer current/basis reads at32960 onward. Locks acquired by context on own switch, original, contract, point/site remain held for the transaction.
3. `customer_draft_v1` calls **another `context_v1`** at32894 after the preceding source read. It subsequently locks cancellation origin, own intent and outbound request at32895–32897 and verifies exact stored bindings. Any earlier own-switch/contract/point/site wait is therefore followed by another current-day calculation; original-context entry time is not a permanent cached deadline authority.
4. The lower actual transport wrapper return chain then reaches `mutate_before_production_contract_source_v1` at18111–18113, which invokes `public.ediel_require_switch_cancellation_source_current_v1` on both prepare and enter when proceed=true.
5. That function prelocks the same cancellation message/source lineage at49173, verifies private original/hash and calls **fresh `context_v1(...,false)` at49195**, then reads/qualifies the same intent/outbound request and requested-method source. Those own rows have already been acquired by the earlier customer-draft guard, so the described own-row wait does not newly occur after this last C context sample.

Thus the proposed enter at22:59:59UTC, wait on its own switch/contract/point/site/intent until23:00:01, and still authorize solely using the initial fixed+1dayD is refuted for this genuine fresh C path: a later reachable context invocation samples fixed+1dayD+1 after the stated waiting locks and fails its window if deadlineD. A test invoking only context_v1 once would not reproduce the complete provider-entry graph.

I inspected the complete generic wrapper delegation/postcheck chain from mutate_v1 down to the base journal function and back, including source/customer guards. This refutes the specific own-row cache hypothesis; it is not a universal theorem that every possible downstream delay or arbitrary unrelated lock is clock-safe. No generic “now() is stale” or BRP intra-day defect is claimed.

## 2. Distinct summer day-domain issue remains a source inconsistency

SQL C context is explicitly TimeZoneUTC and fixed+1 civil date (32818/32822). At a summer22:30UTC instant it observes dayD. TS `stockholmBusinessDate` uses Europe/Stockholm (executionContext197–205), which observesD+1. `canonicalSupplierSwitchSendPolicy` declares C policy calendarEurope/Stockholm atdeadlinePolicy307–319, with L D-4/LKday0 constraints at88–91. These domains differ for an hour each summer night.

Actual transport path is `sendEdielMessageViaSmtp` → `assertRegistryRulebookAllowsSend` atindex.part-2:414 → registry validator/canonical policy. The name `admitEdielOutgoingWire` is absent from these exact published sources; I traced the actual equivalent entry instead. Send guard passes actual row/raw/context and enforces canonical field/application/guide issues. Canonical policy derives current guide referenceDate from Stockholm admission atvalidator359–380. It does not supply C cancellationOfSubtype/own start to the C scheduler window evaluator. Whole-lib call-site search locates `canonicalSupplierSwitchSendPolicyProjection` use in supplierSwitchScheduler policy/window evaluation, not a new sender finalization cutoff on each queued C transport entry.

The authentic originalSubtype/start/deadline do exist in SQL protected cancellation basis and TS switchCancellationSource DTO; mere DTO availability does not prove that registry admission evaluates cancellation deadline. Actual TS `assertEdielAdmissionDateCurrent` atoutboundAttempt37–40/83/88 compares `decision.referenceDate` to fresh Stockholm **guide/admission date**, after awaitenter too. It does not compare original cancellation deadline. At22:30UTC, a fresh admission alreadydatedStockholmD+1 can pass this date-stability check while the fresh SQL cancellation context observes fixed+1D. I found no actual cancellation scheduler call on that provider-entry path that refutes the discrepancy.

This is separate from the failed cached-date hypothesis: repeated fresh SQL contexts fix a wait crossing the fixed+1 boundary but do not convert that date domain into Stockholm. Conversely, a guide-date guard is not automatically a cancellation-window guard.

## 3. Frozen requirement interpretation prevents a confirmed productbug claim

Frozen AT-Z03C-SUPPLIER2904–2911 states L no later than4days before/LK by move-in day, original/object/customer scope, allowed cancellation window, and no completion merely from SMTP250. It does **not explicitly specify a timezone for the cancellation calendar**. ENV-08 rules513–520 explicitly regulates **payload date encoding** and fixed normal-time intervals; it cannot alone prove that all business-calendar deadline dates must use fixed+1. The source's canonical C calendarEurope/Stockholm conflicts with effective SQL context domain, but a pure declared policy is not proof of the actual current transport cutoff or external national-calendar semantics.

Accordingly do not label lawful/late actual dispatch, loosen source gates or silently pick a new timezone on this review alone. Smallest next qualification is to establish the authoritative calendar meaning for the C day boundary and bind it to the existing protected source/send path; only then can a narrowly scoped source correction and meaningful exact-boundary test be chosen if required. This introduces no new LIVE gate, clock override, broad native producer or blanket wait.

## Source pins

## Scope and preservation

Historical accepted projection is returned before fresh transport admission (index.part-2:349 onward), so a timely sent C's later ACK/replay does not acquire a new expiry gate here. The existing transport prepared/enter/current-source cutoff itself is present; its alleged complete absence remains refuted. No changes to accepted histories, late ACK handling, source locks, frozen requirements or current custody.

Source-only fresh analysis; no test/SQL/backend/native/GEN/CI execution, private DB05/source or original/log/archive/artifact acquisition, reservations/refs/docs/PR/merge/source write. Only this own report is written. If an adopted fix later uses this diagnosis, treat reviewer as a diagnosis/evidence contributor rather than counting it as an independent final reviewer of that same fix.
````

`````markdown
# Z03C calendar-window support — reuse existing executable controls first

Conclusion: existing SQL source already contains meaningful L D-4/D-3 and LK day0/expired controls. They call the real cancellation owner context through a real SQL public source function, not a mocked deadline DTO. Therefore absence of those dates from the genuine native31 cases must not become a requirement for four new native runs, time advancement or clock override. Remaining work is exact current-source/execution applicability and, if truly absent, a narrow integrated current guard assertion.

Reviewer `/root/sc031_outbound_peer_b` read only existing public repo source/metadata; sole write is this report. No new tests/SQL/backend/native/GEN/CI execution, original/log/artifact/archive collection, source/ref/docs/claims/publication actions. No whole31 re-audit or previously adjudicated window race re-opened.

## Exact sources

Supplied actual main `d798eb36f35cd4f437d44c8d234b4dbb81adc7a6` / tree `e3b6c11677a2b871a128504f1616def7d22dba1e`; public795 `e49a23d4897f1cb4063a57bf3b1c810321723c3a` / tree `88c04102169c9bd803e2bf4dfb7363ebbc562097`. Independently verified six selected source/test files listed below and actual effective cancellation context/public source/current-source function bodies are identical between these exact targets. No fresh remote/current-CI claim inferred.

Frozen AT-Z03C's timely source cancellation is separate from protocol/profile and reply/restoration effects. This report qualifies only the window-related existing controls and their limits; it does not approve the card.

## Existing meaningful executable calendar assertions

`scripts/ediel-switch-cancellation-sql-regression.mjs` SHA256 `ae817c40ec9d915c85fb65326c5dae0443b705b5c23632e442d023f48dac240e`:

- Lines24–31 load actual tokenizer/original-wire/cancellation source-owner migrations into a finite PGlite component, with explicit reduced tables and named actor/legal/original/provider/receipt ports.
- Line40 `read()` executes actual `SELECT ediel_switch_cancellation_source_v1(...)`; the loaded public wrapper calls actual `gridex_switch_cancellations.context_v1(...)`.
- Lines51–57 `originalScope(reason,offset)` change only declared fixture original bytes/hash, own switch start date and original receipt object/hash; genuine production originals are not touched.
- Line58 selects actual Z22 original/start offset+4 and asserts source status authorized.
- Line59 selects Z22/+3 and asserts exact missing `source_z03_cancellation_calendar_window`.
- Line60 selects Z23/0 and asserts authorized.
- Line61 selects Z23/-1 and asserts the same exact calendar refusal.

These are behavior checks of the actual date/source guard using independently selected fixture reason/date values. They do not merely assign `deadline` or a precomputed false result. The test dates use existing DB `(now()+interval '1 hour')::date`; context uses `(clock_timestamp()+interval '1 hour')::date`. No caller clock/date override or native backend waiting occurs. I do not infer timezone conformance from that expression: session/environment timezone and authentic clock applicability remain explicit qualifications, not an assumption that every cast is a fixed-zone legal day.

## Limits of reuse on current effective source

Actual d798/e49 context body SHA256 `600e83702ed4695229aeaa133b258867d9736ac1939b8196e7d2e56ca145aa4e`. It differs from the script's initial20260930214933 context predecessor by the added call `gridex_switch_cancellations.prelock_customer_source_v1(c,sw)` before source discovery. Removing that one added statement yields the unchanged predecessor body; calendar clause/start-day/original-Z22/Z23 predicate are exact. The four boundary probes execute before later20261007132500 original-method augmentation is loaded. Thus they are reusable evidence for the actual unchanged calendar/source-context mechanics with their declared ports, not an execution of the complete current customer/method/transport authority chain.

Current public source wrapper adds original-method qualification; current source/send guard adds fresh customer/method binding. The selected component also tests separate private origin/current refusal, but does not combine every calendar offset with all current wrappers. Do not silently promote historical source-window execution into current integrated owner/send execution.

Current transport production-contract wrapper first obtains the underlying mutation result and calls `ediel_require_switch_cancellation_source_current_v1` only for prepare/enter with proceed=true. The script's `fixtureProceed:false` accepted replay controls at83/167 return before that branch. They prove replay preservation, not expired current-send rejection. Direct current guard tests cover revoked/cancelled/source/method scenarios elsewhere, but not those four calendar offsets in the inspected script.
`````

`````javascript
import{createHash}from'node:crypto';import assert from'node:assert/strict';import{fileURLToPath}from'node:url';import{dirname,resolve}from'node:path';
const root=dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)),resultPath=resolve(root,'results.json');

const hash=b=>createHash('sha256').update(b).digest('hex'),bindings=JSON.parse(readFileSync(resolve(root,'source-bindings.json'),'utf8'));
const result={version:1,startedAt:new Date().toISOString(),source:bindings.head,tree:bindings.tree,base:bindings.base,runtime:{node:process.version,nodeBinarySha256:hash(readFileSync(process.execPath)),pgliteVersion:JSON.parse(readFileSync('/workspace/gridex-c3-20261012/node_modules/@electric-sql/pglite/package.json','utf8')).version},status:'RUNNING',oldChecks:null,oldStatus:'NOT_COMPLETED',newStatus:'NOT_COMPLETED',newControls:[]};
writeFileSync(resultPath,JSON.stringify(result,null,2)+'\n');globalThis[Symbol.for('ediel.c03.finite')]=result;
let boundary;

 assert.equal(process.version,bindings.runtime.expectedNodeVersion);assert.equal(result.runtime.nodeBinarySha256,bindings.runtime.expectedNodeSha256);assert.equal(result.runtime.pgliteVersion,'0.3.14');
 for(const item of bindings.literalDependencies)assert.equal(hash(readFileSync(resolve(root,item.repositoryPath))),item.sha256);
 assert.equal(hash(readFileSync(resolve(root,'scripts/original-public.mjs'))),bindings.original.sha256);
 assert.equal(hash(readFileSync(resolve(root,'current-context.sql'))),bindings.currentContext.sha256);
 for(const[path,expected]of Object.entries(bindings.temporaryFiles))assert.equal(hash(readFileSync(resolve(root,path))),expected);
 process.env.EDIEL_PGLITE_MODULE=bindings.runtime.pgliteModule;process.env.EDIEL_SQL_REPOSITORY=root;
 boundary=await import('./runtime-boundary.mjs');
 console.log('FINITE_RUNTIME',JSON.stringify(result.runtime));
 await import('./program.mjs');
 assert.equal(result.oldStatus,'PASS');assert.equal(result.newStatus,'PASS');assert.equal(result.newControls.length,4);assert.deepEqual(boundary.attempts,[]);
 result.status='PASS';result.exitCode=0;
}catch(error){result.status='FAIL';result.exitCode=1;result.error={name:error.name,message:error.message,stack:error.stack};console.error(error);process.exitCode=1;}
finally{result.boundaryAttempts=boundary?.attempts??[];result.completedAt=new Date().toISOString();result.bindingsSha256=hash(readFileSync(resolve(root,'source-bindings.json')));result.files={};for(const path of ['controller.mjs','runtime-boundary.mjs','program.mjs','scripts/original-public.mjs','current-context.sql'])result.files[path]=hash(readFileSync(resolve(root,path)));writeFileSync(resultPath,JSON.stringify(result,null,2)+'\n');console.log('FINITE_RESULT',JSON.stringify({status:result.status,oldChecks:result.oldChecks,oldStatus:result.oldStatus,newCount:result.newControls.length,newStatus:result.newStatus,boundaryAttempts:result.boundaryAttempts.length,exitCode:result.exitCode}));}
`````

`````javascript
export const attempts=[];
const deny=label=>function(...args){attempts.push({kind:label});throw Error('FINITE_BOUNDARY_DENIED:'+label)};
for(const[name,keys]of[
 ['node:child_process',['spawn','spawnSync','exec','execSync','execFile','execFileSync','fork']],
 ['node:net',['connect','createConnection','createServer']],['node:tls',['connect','createServer']],

 ['node:dns',['lookup','resolve','resolve4','resolve6','reverse','lookupService']],

}

const net=require('node:net');for(const k of ['connect','listen']){if(k in net.Socket.prototype)net.Socket.prototype[k]=deny('net.Socket.'+k);if(k in net.Server.prototype)net.Server.prototype[k]=deny('net.Server.'+k)}
`````
